## 2026-09-20 01:58 CEST — Existing-data, treatment, and panel feasibility checkpoints

- **Task:** Resume and execute the first evidence gates in `prompts/main_research_prompt.md` using the supplied `data_raw/` files.
- **Reason:** Determine what can be supported empirically before collecting lower-priority features or fitting models.
- **Input:** Eleven immutable raw files covering hotel statistics, Bergfex resort listings, lift geometries, existing cluster assignments/summaries, and legacy maps.
- **Method:** Hashed and profiled every raw file; inspected the earlier extraction logic; built stable derived tables; decoded the GPKG and independently recomputed listing-to-cluster distances; queried the official current municipality layer for all 271 resort points with raw response caching; cached official OFS metadata and Magic Pass press evidence; manually transcribed page-referenced membership events; extracted the current official destination JSON; generated feasibility and data-quality assessments; normalized source metadata and the data dictionary; added and ran automated integrity tests.
- **Result:** The raw files contain 271 resort listings, 242 lift clusters, 1,805 uniquely assigned valid lift geometries, 186 hotel municipalities, and 28,455 observed municipality-month hotel-night values from 2013-01 to 2026-03. Point municipality is resolved for 268 listings, with 103 listings reaching 74 OFS hotel municipalities. Official evidence records 93 membership-related events, including 88 base entries; only 18 base-entry events have both a candidate listing link and point-municipality hotel coverage, representing 14 municipalities. Eight tests pass and the checkpoint pipeline reruns end to end from cached evidence.
- **Problems encountered:** The 2021 and 2026 Magic Pass PDFs repeatedly returned incomplete downloads, though the official archive HTML was cached. Twenty-nine official event labels are unresolved to the supplied listing table. The current map embeds 95 destinations while the page/press headline refers to more than 100. Three resort coordinates fall outside the Swiss current municipality layer. The upstream version of the lift GPKG and retrieval dates of legacy extracts remain unknown. Matplotlib initially tried to write its cache outside the workspace; the script now uses an ignored workspace cache.
- **Decision:** Preserve the existing clustering as a provisional reference layer; do not rebuild it. Treat point containment only as descriptive linkage, never causal exposure. Keep every membership event `treatment_ready = false`. Select Path C at the current checkpoint and reject premature DiD, causal forests, meta-learners, neural networks, uplift scores, and business recommendations. The effective sample is the number of destinations/municipalities, not monthly rows.
- **Next step:** Review the 18 candidate treatment/outcome links and shared municipalities, define independent destination scope, complete season-by-season membership/exit evidence, construct explicit municipality exposure weights, and audit control contamination before reassessing causal feasibility.

## 2026-09-20 10:51 CEST — Official hotel-capacity integration

- **Task:** Continue the main research mission by collecting and integrating monthly hotel supply and occupancy information from the official HESTA municipality table.
- **Reason:** Separate changes in hotel demand from contemporaneous changes in establishments, rooms, and beds before considering any treatment-effect design.
- **Input:** OFS PXWeb table `px-x-1003020000_201`, its live metadata, the existing 31,248-row legacy hotel-demand panel, reviewed destination scopes, and provisional membership anchors.
- **Method:** Cached the official metadata; split an initially rejected full-table request into 14 annual POST queries; preserved every exact request, raw response, timestamp, checksum, and collection-log row; parsed source tokens without imputation; joined the current table one-to-one to the legacy demand grid; aggregated capacity only over complete reviewed municipal scopes; retained official occupancy rates only for single-municipality destinations; computed a flagged 24-month adjacent-window capacity diagnostic with causal interpretation disabled.
- **Result:** The capacity panel has 31,248 municipality-month rows for 186 reference municipalities. Establishments, rooms, and beds are complete in 29,274 rows. The reviewed destination panel retains 1,848 rows, with 1,637 complete capacity scopes. Eight of 11 panel units have both 24 observed pre and 24 active-post capacity months under the explicit continuity assumption. Provenance now has 43 source records, the dictionary has 345 rows, the full offline pipeline succeeds, and 13 tests pass.
- **Problems encountered:** The API rejected the 248,000-cell request with HTTP 403; annual chunks succeeded. The live table differs from the legacy extraction for arrivals and overnight stays in three Davos months in 2014 (six cells), so the legacy series remains the primary outcome and the revisions are reported separately. The source publishes 59 bed-occupancy rates above 100; these values are retained unchanged and flagged rather than corrected by assumption.
- **Decision:** Treat establishments, rooms, and beds as time-varying supply covariates. Use live-table demand only for internally consistent capacity-adjusted ratios. Do not average official occupancy percentages across municipalities. The capacity diagnostic is descriptive only and changes no causal-readiness flag; Path C remains in force.
- **Next step:** Collect snow/weather covariates, continue membership-history completion, resolve remaining destination labels, and audit historical donor contamination and spillovers before reconsidering a causal design.

## 2026-09-20 11:08 CEST — SLF snow-source selection and proxy panel

- **Task:** Compare authoritative snow/climate sources and build the first historically aligned snow-vulnerability proxy without claiming direct piste measurement.
- **Reason:** Snow reliability is central to the thesis and a material time-varying confounder/strategic-need dimension, but Alpine topography makes undocumented nearest-station assumptions scientifically weak.
- **Input:** Official SLF data-service conditions, archive README, 251-row IMIS station catalogue, daily-file directory, and the 1,212,878-row consolidated daily snow file; MeteoSwiss SwissMetNet and Copernicus ERA5-Land official documentation; 271 resort points and published top altitudes; reviewed treatment-unit configuration.
- **Method:** Compared authority, historical/geographic coverage, machine readability, licence and spatial limitations. Selected SLF IMIS for direct mountain snow-depth observations; retained MeteoSwiss for later temperature/precipitation and ERA5-Land as a gridded sensitivity source. Parsed daily HS and modelled HN_1D, treated negative physical depths as invalid without changing raw data, built monthly and November–April panels, required 80% longitudinal winter coverage before station selection, assigned the geographically nearest passing station, and retained distance/elevation-gap quality diagnostics. Multi-station destination months are averaged only when every assigned station passes 80% daily coverage.
- **Result:** Of 166 SLF snow stations with daily data, 115 pass the 2013/14–2025/26 winter gate. The 271 resort links comprise 112 high-, 99 moderate- and 60 low-comparability proxies; 211 resorts have high/moderate links and at least ten usable winters. The reviewed panel has 1,733 complete snow-proxy months, including 1,565 with an observed hotel outcome. Provenance now has 48 source records, the data dictionary 359 rows, and 14 tests pass.
- **Problems encountered:** The raw archive contains 2,295 negative HS values among snow-station records; these are physically invalid and become missing only in derived fields. SLF warns that some data are raw and not regularly corrected. IMIS sites are designed for avalanche monitoring, often above treeline, and can differ from pistes in aspect, wind, terrain, grooming and snowmaking. Sixty resort links fail the high/moderate spatial-elevation thresholds.
- **Decision:** Use the SLF series only as a transparent external mountain-station proxy. Label HN_1D as modelled new snow, keep `direct_slope_representation = false` everywhere, give low-comparability links only partial data-quality credit, and leave every snow causal-approval flag false. Path C remains unchanged.
- **Next step:** Add official MeteoSwiss temperature/precipitation, test snow sensitivity to alternate stations or ERA5-Land, and continue membership/donor evidence audits before any causal estimation.

## 2026-09-20 11:35 CEST — MeteoSwiss temperature and precipitation proxy panel

- **Task:** Add historically aligned regional temperature and precipitation proxies for the reviewed destination units.
- **Reason:** Weather is a material time-varying confounder for monthly hotel demand and remained missing after the hotel-capacity and SLF snow milestones.
- **Input:** Official SwissMetNet documentation, FSDI STAC metadata, parameter catalogue, 158-station catalogue, 19,546-row station-parameter inventory, station-item response, seven historical daily station files, seven recent-year files, 15 reviewed resort components, and the destination-unit configuration.
- **Method:** Screened the official inventory for current stations with daily mean/minimum/maximum 2 m air temperature and 06:00-to-06:00 UTC precipitation starting by 2013 and no recorded end date; selected the nearest eligible station for each reviewed component; checksum-validated and concatenated historical/recent files; rejected duplicate dates and invalid temperature ordering; aggregated monthly observations with an 80% coverage gate; calculated station/calendar-month 2013–2025 analytical normals and anomalies; deduplicated shared stations within destinations; withheld destination metrics unless every expected station-month passed; retained distance, station elevation, elevation gap, precipitation window and non-piste representation flags.
- **Result:** The inventory yields 112 eligible stations, while the 15 reviewed resort components require seven. The selected raw files contain 265,838 rows over their full histories and 33,866 aligned daily rows from 2013-01 through 2026-03. All temperature values are complete and ordered; 27 precipitation values are missing. Of 1,113 station-months, 1,111 pass the gate. Of 1,749 reviewed destination-months, 1,744 are complete. The main 1,848-row outcome panel has 1,576 rows with both observed hotel nights and complete weather, and 1,560 with observed outcome plus complete snow and weather. Provenance now has 55 source records and the data dictionary 375 rows.
- **Problems encountered:** EVO in 2016-07 and INT in 2013-08 have only 17 and 18 valid precipitation days, below the 80% threshold. The official precipitation interval crosses calendar-day boundaries. Several stations are far below published resort-top altitude, so absolute levels cannot be interpreted as piste microclimate.
- **Decision:** Use SwissMetNet as a transparent regional weather/anomaly proxy only. Base horizontal comparability on distance while retaining elevation gaps separately. Do not impute the two incomplete station-months, do not relabel the 06:00-to-06:00 precipitation interval, keep all direct-slope and causal-approval flags false, and retain Path C.
- **Next step:** Complete the 25 unverified membership seasons and 26 unresolved entry labels, then perform alternative-station/gridded climate sensitivity and a documented donor membership/spillover audit before reconsidering causal estimation.

## 2026-09-21 11:25 CEST — Entity-scope screen and annual winter/summer register

- **Task:** Review all unmatched historical Magic Pass labels and test the user's proposal to reconstruct membership and winter/summer scope from each annual launch publication.
- **Reason:** Unresolved labels contaminated the apparent donor pool, while the existing continuity table did not distinguish winter-only from summer-winter access.
- **Input:** The official Magic Pass launch dossiers and complete annual maps for 2017/2018, 2018/2019, 2022/2023, 2023/2024 and 2025/2026; the Magic Pass branded 2024/2025 map distributed by Raiffeisen; the recovered 2021/2022 official dossier; current official map; supplied resort directory; official Aeschi and Vallée de Joux scope pages.
- **Method:** Rendered and visually inspected the relevant PDF pages and legends; transcribed only individually named winter/summer symbols; preserved mixed-component destinations; classified all 26 unmatched entry labels in a versioned review; expanded only explicit non-equivalent scope relations; excluded their point municipalities conservatively from the donor pool; added page/checksum/output tests and regenerated provenance.
- **Result:** Fifty-two active destination-unit seasons across six annual publications now have individual modality evidence; 31 have summer access for at least one published component and 21 are winter-only or part of the founding winter-only pass. All 26 unmatched labels are classified: 10 foreign, eight composite components, three partial/overlapping, one ambiguous and four Swiss missing/unverified. Four additional municipalities are excluded from the clean donor upper bound, reducing it from 57 to 53 and reducing mechanical donor pairs from 438 to 413. The 2021 dossier downloaded successfully but supplies only aggregate 40-winter/21-summer counts, so seven continuity gaps remain. Provenance has 65 records and the data dictionary 422 rows.

These are stored as scope relations, never automatic identities. Blank CSV label lists are read as missing by pandas and required explicit handling in the validation test.
- **Decision:** Use annual launch lists/maps as the preferred season-membership and modality source when the publication is complete and dated. Do not infer absence from partial releases, carry summer status backward, flatten mixed domains or promote scope relations to causal mappings. Path C and all zero causal-approval flags remain unchanged.
- **Validation:** Full offline pipeline generated all derived tables; the final 18-test suite passed. PDF page bounds, cached checksums, 52 offering rows, 31 summer flags, 26 scope classifications, 14 relation rows, nine related listings, four additional donor exclusions and zero causal approvals are asserted.
- **Next step:** Resolve the seven remaining active unit-season gaps, four missing/unverified Swiss listing relations and the ambiguous Les Diablerets scope; then perform a historical membership/spillover audit for donors around Axalp, Bumbach, Reichenbach and Saas-Fee before considering any causal model.

## 2026-09-21 16:20 CEST — Temporal controls and SLF alternative-station sensitivity

- **Task:** Complete the temporal-confounding preparation requested by the mission and test whether the selected SLF snow proxies are robust to a simple alternative-station rule.
- **Reason:** Three donor pre-windows overlap the broad COVID disruption period, calendar effects were not yet explicit in the reviewed panel, and nearest-station snow levels can be sensitive to Alpine topography.
- **Input:** The 2013-01 to 2026-12 monthly analysis grid, reviewed destination panel, versioned temporal-control configuration, 115 SLF stations passing the longitudinal gate, their 2013/14–2025/26 winter aggregates, and the 15 resort components in the reviewed outcome scope.
- **Method:** Built deterministic month, quarter, May–April pass-season, November–April winter, December/January peak and Gregorian Easter fields; flagged March 2020–April 2022 as a deliberately broad sensitivity interval without claiming a canton-specific legal chronology; joined the fields many-to-one to every destination-month. For each reviewed resort component, excluded the selected SLF station, selected the next-nearest station passing the same 80% longitudinal gate, restricted comparisons to winters usable at both stations, and compared winter-depth correlation, absolute/relative level gaps, ≥30 cm reliability shares and linear-trend direction.
- **Result:** All 1,848 destination-month rows receive temporal controls and 286 fall in the broad COVID interval. All 15 reviewed resort components have 13 common usable winters in the station sensitivity. Six comparisons have high, five moderate and four low descriptive agreement; median winter-depth correlation is 0.864, median relative mean absolute depth difference is 47.2%, and trend direction agrees for 11 components. The source register now has 69 records and the data dictionary 447 rows.
- **Problems encountered:** The first temporal test referenced a script-local boolean helper and failed with `NameError`; a test-local normaliser fixed the test without changing data. High co-movement can coexist with large level differences, so correlation alone was not accepted as proxy validation.
- **Decision:** Calendar and broad COVID fields are diagnostic controls only; later event-study work must show full-sample, broad-exclusion and alternative-period specifications. The next-nearest-station test reduces one uncertainty but does not approve any snow covariate: four components have low agreement and no alternative is a piste observation. An independent gridded snow sensitivity and regional-weather sensitivity remain required. Path C and all causal approval flags remain unchanged.
- **Validation:** The complete offline pipeline succeeded end to end. Twenty-one automated tests passed, including the temporal partition/join, alternative-station distinctness, agreement counts, checksum lineage and zero-causal-approval assertions.
- **Next step:** Audit historical membership, competing passes, accessibility, investment and spillovers for the 35 shortlisted donor pairs; resolve the remaining Swiss entity/scope gaps; then add gridded snow and regional-weather sensitivities before reconsidering a case-study causal design.

## 2026-09-21 17:05 CEST — Network-wide donor proximity and same-destination distance gradient

- **Task:** Strengthen the donor spillover audit and implement the researcher's proposal to compare different hotel municipalities by distance to the same treated station/destination.
- **Reason:** The prior donor filter measured distance only to the treatment under review, so a nominal donor could remain close to another Magic Pass destination. The municipality outcome scope also lacked a reproducible distance-gradient diagnostic holding the treated destination fixed.
- **Input:** Fifty-two apparent-control municipalities, 35 shortlisted treatment-donor pairs, all historically/currently resolved Magic resort points, 271 supplied resort coordinates, seven continuity-plus-24/24 treatment cases, 186 HESTA municipality series, and the official swissBOUNDARIES3D 2026-01 GeoPackage ZIP.
- **Method:** Computed the minimum haversine distance from every apparent donor's resort points to every resolved Magic resort point and retained 20/30/50 km sensitivity flags plus shared-cluster risk. Checksum-cached the 37.4 MB official boundary archive; parsed the EPSG:2056 GeoPackage directly; mapped all hotel series to current polygons; retained representative-point, centroid and minimum-polygon distances to each reviewed destination's component points; built five common distance bands; and compared municipal 24-month pre/post log changes within the same destination. A buffered near/far contrast uses ≤30 km versus >50 km and excludes 30–50 km.
- **Result:** Twenty-three of 52 apparent controls are within 30 km of a resolved Magic point. Among the 35 shortlisted pairs, 15 are within 30 km and 28 within 50 km; only seven lie beyond 50 km. The distance crosswalk has 2,046 rows (11 destinations × 186 hotel series). Seven treatment cases yield 1,302 diagnostic rows, of which 1,246 have at least 18 observed months in both windows. Six of seven descriptive near-minus-far contrasts are positive; Moléson is the exception.
- **Problems encountered:** HESTA contains both Moutier (BE), BFS 700, and Moutier (JU), BFS 6831, while the 2026 boundary product contains one current BFS 6831 polygon. Both outcome series are preserved, their shared current geometry is explicit, and the 700-to-6831 bridge is recorded rather than treated as full historical harmonisation. An initial one-to-one geometry merge correctly failed and was replaced by a documented many-to-one boundary-version bridge.
- **Decision:** Use network-wide proximity to prioritise donor review, not as an automatic causal exclusion. Use the same-destination gradient as an exposure/scope diagnostic only. Current polygons, representative points, missing hotel coordinates/travel times, cross-destination contamination, COVID and simultaneous investments prevent causal interpretation. All control, exposure and effect flags remain false; Path C is unchanged.
- **Validation:** The complete offline pipeline succeeded. Twenty-three automated tests passed, covering all 52 apparent controls, all 35 shortlisted pairs, the 11×186 distance grid, the Moutier bridge, six positive descriptive contrasts and zero causal approvals. Provenance now contains 72 source records and 459 dictionary rows.
- **Next step:** Deprioritise the 15 shortlisted pairs within 30 km of another resolved Magic point, then audit historical membership, competing-pass participation, accessibility and investment for the remaining candidates. If authoritative routing or accommodation-coordinate data can be obtained, replace representative-point distance with travel-time or hotel-weighted exposure sensitivity.

## 2026-09-21 17:26 CEST — Remote-donor competing-pass and local-shock audit

- **Task:** Continue the donor audit for the seven shortlisted treatment-donor pairs lying beyond 50 km from every resolved Magic Pass point.
- **Reason:** Geographic separation alone does not establish a clean counterfactual. Competing regional passes, cross-border domains and donor-specific tourism investments can produce independent changes in hotel demand.

## 2026-09-22 00:42 CEST — Checkpoints 4–5 closed and Path C v0.1 implemented

- **Task:** Formalise causal/HTE feasibility decisions and implement the first reproducible exploratory segmentation and analogue decision-support architecture.
- **Reason:** Entity-scope and donor audits are complete, but zero treatments and controls are causally approved. The project needed to advance without converting monthly repetition or unadjusted outcomes into false uplift predictions.
- **Input:** The 271 supplied resort listings, preserved 162 linked lift clusters, current and historical Magic evidence, scope relations, resort data-quality table, SLF vulnerability proxies, and eight reviewed destinations with complete adjacent 24-month capacity diagnostics.
- **Method:** Chose the existing lift cluster as the least duplicated available entity; aggregated every listing's structural features with min/median/max retained; excluded membership, outcomes, snow and geography from structural similarity; standardised top altitude and log1p ski-area/lift counts; fitted deterministic k-means for k=2–8; applied minimum-size and 80% subsample-ARI gates before choosing the highest silhouette; built three-neighbour current-Magic and reviewed-treated analogues; calibrated support flags from leave-one-out reference distances; kept the analogue opportunity percentile separate from the snow-vulnerability percentile; left uplift, intervals and composite score blank.
- **Result:** Of 162 linked clusters, 156 have complete structural features and 53 show disagreement among multiple assigned listings. The selected k=2 solution has silhouette 0.514, median bootstrap ARI 0.987 and p10 ARI 0.924, with segments of 83 and 73 clusters. Fifty structurally complete current-Magic clusters and eight reviewed treated destinations form the reference pools. The prototype scores 104 apparent candidates; 74 have good treated-reference support, five moderate extrapolation and 25 strong extrapolation. All 104 have two plotted descriptive coordinates, but zero predicted-uplift values and zero composite scores.
- **Problems encountered:** The directory features have no observation date, so they cannot be called pre-treatment. The eight analogue outcome patterns are unadjusted adjacent-window changes and generate a discrete opportunity signal. Lift clusters reduce listing duplication but are not independently validated destination domains. Tourism dependence and accessibility are unavailable at an approved cluster exposure scope.
- **Decision:** Formally fail Checkpoint 4 for current causal identification and Checkpoint 5 for heterogeneous-effect ML. Select Path C v0.1 only as current structural segmentation plus descriptive analogue support. Do not fit gradient boosting/random forest without a defensible supervised target. Do not rank candidates with a composite score. Strong-OOD candidates must not receive confident interpretation.
- **Validation:** The pipeline now rebuilds the feature matrix, segment diagnostics, analogues, decision-support export and two figures before provenance. Provenance contains 92 source records and the dictionary 754 table-variable rows. All 27 automated tests pass, covering entity counts, feature timing warning, candidate exclusions, k-selection/stability, exact segment sizes, reference integrity, OOD counts, and deliberately blank uplift/composite fields.
- **Next step:** Add authoritative accessibility, tourism-dependence and competing-pass features; test cluster aggregation sensitivity; seek dated infrastructure snapshots; and complete independent snow/weather robustness before upgrading the decision framework.

## 2026-09-22 00:54 CEST — Path C aggregation sensitivity and confidence qualification

All 15 component nodes have an identified anchor URL, but zero historical stop/service comparisons have been executed; no line-stability conclusion is claimed.

- **Input:** Checksum-verified ARE NPVM 2025 traffic-zone geometry, swissBOUNDARIES3D 2026 municipality geometry, the exact point-municipality crosswalk, listing-level transport context and cluster exact-only medians.
- **Method:** Recomputed every point, municipality intersection, eligible zone set, deterministic nearest-zone tie-break and distance in EPSG:2056. Compared all-usable and exact-only medians only for clusters containing both assignment types; kept historical lineage and feature approval as separate false gates.
- **Result:** All 98 assignments and distances reproduce exactly. Median proxy distance is 0.64 km and maximum 3.64 km; 38 are within 0.5 km, 35 within 0.5–1 km, 19 within 1–2 km and six exceed 2 km. Seventy-three clusters use a proxy; 50 are proxy-only, including 28 apparent candidates. Across 23 mixed clusters, public-transport major-centre median sensitivity is 2.0 minutes at the median, 11.3 at p90 and 20.0 maximum.
- **Problems encountered:** Most proxy-using clusters cannot be internally compared because they have no exactly contained listing point. Geometry reconstruction validates the algorithm, not the traffic-zone travel time as an observed resort route.
- **Decision:** Adopt Path C v0.9 while retaining every transport-accessibility approval as false. Expose proxy-only dependence and do not turn successful geometric reconstruction into route, historical or causal validation.
- **Validation:** The complete offline pipeline reused the GTFS routing caches, regenerated all dependent outputs, produced 115 provenance records and a 1,327-row data dictionary, and passed all 36 automated tests.
- **Next step:** Compare the identified 2018–2025 GTFS anchors where access is permitted, then add motorway/cross-border population, destination catchments, competing-pass status and dated infrastructure evidence.

## 2026-09-23 02:19 CEST — Same-destination distance versus scheduled transit

- **Task:** Compare geographic distance and scheduled public-transport time for the same municipalities around each reviewed destination, following the researcher's proposal that transit time may be more informative than crow-flight distance.
- **Reason:** The distance gradient and GTFS journey modules used the same 2,046 destination–municipality universe but had not been compared directly, leaving the degree of proxy disagreement unquantified.
- **Input:** Current 2026 representative-point and minimum-polygon distances, winter-2026 outbound/return journey summaries, main municipality stops and reviewed component arrival nodes.
- **Method:** Exact one-to-one merge on destination and municipality; preserve all coverage states; calculate within-destination percentile ranks and Spearman-equivalent rank correlations without a SciPy dependency; flag absolute rank gaps of at least 0.25; summarise the five existing distance bands.
- **Result:** Of 2,046 pairs, 1,700 have both outbound and return means and 1,104 are complete at every sampled time. Ten destinations support bidirectional correlations: median 0.743, minimum 0.607 for Saas-Fee and maximum 0.869 for Les Pléiades. Two hundred twenty-five pairs have materially worse transit rank than distance suggests; 171 have materially better transit rank. Meiringen-Hasliberg has no bidirectional mean under the declared limits.
- **Problems encountered:** SciPy is not installed, so rank correlation is computed transparently as Pearson correlation of average ranks. Four destinations have no fully complete pair universe; partial and complete correlations remain separate. Current geometry and two reference days cannot establish historical exposure.
- **Decision:** Adopt Path C v0.10 and retain both distance and scheduled time as separate descriptive diagnostics. Do not substitute one for the other, create an accessibility score, or approve causal exposure/effects.
- **Validation:** The complete offline pipeline reused both GTFS caches, regenerated all dependent outputs, produced 116 provenance records and a 1,384-row data dictionary, and passed all 37 automated tests.
- **Next step:** Compare historical GTFS anchors where access is permitted; otherwise continue with motorway/cross-border population, destination-catchment and competing-pass evidence.

## 2026-09-23 10:55 CEST — Harmonised cross-border population context

- **Task:** Close the known geographic truncation of the Swiss-only population-radius diagnostic without converting residential population into an assumed ski-destination catchment.
- **Reason:** The prior STATPOP municipality-point calculation showed that 112, 158, 162 and 162 cluster circles at 30/60/90/120 km intersect the national boundary. The project's next-evidence list therefore required consistent foreign population rather than treating the Swiss border as the edge of the market.
- **Historical-GTFS gate:** Rechecked the configured 2018–2025 official archive URLs and the locally preserved archive-domain `robots.txt`. The domain states `User-agent: *` and `Disallow: /`; no direct ZIP was fetched. Historical stop/service stability remains unverified and now explicitly requires manual or otherwise authorised acquisition.
- **Input:** Official Eurostat GISCO Census Grid 2021 version 3, released 30 May 2026. The 566,125,577-byte multi-format ZIP advertises byte ranges. Its central directory showed that the required CSV member is 49,110,575 bytes compressed / 339,625,996 bytes uncompressed with CRC32 `54cdc404`; the official README is 2,390 / 5,428 bytes with CRC32 `8653c53b`.
- **Collection:** Added a resumable byte-range collector that checks `robots.txt`, validates `Content-Range`, archive ETag, compressed and uncompressed sizes, ZIP CRC32 and final SHA-256. The full unfiltered CSV member was preserved with SHA-256 `fe54d920ebbdb35c2b48f75276cef63d9dd302591ca70deb103351b2a621b5f1`; the README was preserved separately and its definitions of `T`, EPSG:3035, `-8888`, `-9999` and `CC_unallocated` are asserted before processing.
- **Method:** Parsed lower-left grid coordinates from the official INSPIRE cell identifier, represented each 1 km square at its centroid, and summed observed 2021 total population within the predeclared 30/60/90/120 km radii of all 162 linked cluster coordinates. Preserved Switzerland-only, foreign-only and mixed Swiss/foreign reporting scopes; did not split mixed cells, impute unavailable totals or allocate national unplaced records. Kept the 2021 grid separate from STATPOP 2025 municipality-point totals.
- **Result:** The raw CSV contains 4,595,932 rows, of which 4,595,919 are spatial grid cells. Thirteen unallocated country records represent 434,787 observed people across the full source universe and remain excluded from spatial totals. The regional extract contains 181,598 cells, all with observed total population. Foreign-only population occurs within 30 km for 99 clusters / 73 candidates and within 120 km for all 162 / 104. Across clusters, the median foreign-only share is 20.6% at 60 km and 36.7% at 120 km.
- **Decision:** Adopt Path C v0.11 with `eurostat_2021_one_km_population_context_not_travel_time_or_destination_catchment`. This resolves geographic cross-border truncation, not visitor-market identification. Cell-centroid error is bounded at 0.707 km; mixed-border population remains separate; all feature approvals remain false; `accessibility_score`, predicted uplift and composite score stay blank.
- **Validation:** The complete offline pipeline regenerated every dependent table and report. All 38 automated tests pass, including exact country-scope population partitions, monotonic radii, 162-cluster/104-candidate coverage, unallocated-record handling and all previous causal/non-scoring gates. Provenance now contains 119 source records and 1,513 table-variable rows.
- **Next step:** Add motorway proximity, then continue destination-catchment, competing-pass, dated-infrastructure and gridded climate evidence. Historical GTFS comparison remains contingent on a manual or authorised archive channel.

## 2026-09-24 01:00 CEST — SwissMetNet next-nearest-station sensitivity

- **Task:** Test whether the regional temperature and precipitation anomalies used for the 15 reviewed resort components are robust to replacing every selected SwissMetNet station with the next-nearest distinct eligible station.
- **Reason:** The primary weather proxy used the nearest station satisfying the four-parameter and start-date gates, but station choice had not been subjected to the explicit sensitivity already applied to snow. This closes the alternative-station part of the weather evidence gap without claiming independent gridded validation.
- **Input:** The same official MeteoSwiss station inventory and daily mean/minimum/maximum temperature plus precipitation definitions used for the primary proxy; 14 newly collected checksum-verified historical/current files for CHD, GIH, LAG, ORO, SIO, THU and ZER; six existing files reused for EVO, INT and MVE; the 15 reviewed component points; and the 11-unit reviewed destination mapping.
- **Method:** Selected the next-nearest distinct station passing the unchanged four-parameter and no-end-date/start-by-2013 gates; applied the same 80% daily-to-monthly coverage requirement; calculated alternative station-specific 2013–2025 calendar-month normals and anomalies; compared selected and alternative component series on common valid months; aggregated components to the unchanged destination units; and classified agreement with predeclared high/moderate thresholds of 0.90/0.80 for temperature-anomaly correlation and 0.70/0.50 for precipitation-anomaly correlation. No output is promoted to a causal feature.
- **Result:** Ten alternative stations support all 15 components. Their panel contains 1,590 station-months from 2013-01 through 2026-03, with 1,583 passing all four coverage gates. Component agreement is high for 10 links and moderate for five; median temperature-anomaly correlation is 0.967 with median absolute difference 0.287 °C, and median precipitation-anomaly correlation is 0.815 with median absolute difference 18.70 mm/month. The 11-destination panel contains 1,749 rows, 1,737 with both proxies complete; eight destinations have high and three moderate agreement, with median correlations of 0.959 for temperature and 0.885 for precipitation.
- **Problems encountered:** The first network collection attempts failed inside the restricted sandbox and remain recorded in the append-only collection log; approved official downloads then succeeded. The initial builder incorrectly expected all alternatives to be new files, but EVO, INT and MVE were already present in the primary seven-station cache because they serve other components. The implementation now combines the primary and alternative manifests, requires exactly the 10 needed station codes and records all 20 reused/new station files in provenance.
- **Decision:** Adopt Path C v0.16 with `selected_vs_next_nearest_eligible_swissmetnet_regional_weather_proxy_sensitivity_not_piste_microclimate_gridded_validation_or_causal_approval`. Treat the result as evidence that the regional anomaly signal is not highly fragile to eligible-station choice, while retaining the need for an independent gridded climate product. Keep every weather-sensitivity and causal approval false and leave predicted uplift, accessibility and composite fields blank.
- **Validation:** The complete offline pipeline regenerated all dependent outputs. All 43 automated tests pass, including exact 10-station/15-component grain, 1,583 usable station-months, 1,737 complete paired destination-months, high/moderate agreement classifications and protected-output gates. Provenance contains 132 source records and the data dictionary contains 2,008 table-variable rows.
- **Next step:** Add an independent gridded regional-weather sensitivity, and independently retain the gridded-snow, destination-catchment, authorised historical-GTFS, historical competing-pass and dated-infrastructure evidence gates.

## 2026-09-24 01:51 CEST — MeteoSwiss 1 km monthly-grid weather sensitivity

- **Task:** Test the reviewed components' SwissMetNet temperature and precipitation anomalies against an official gridded regional product, while preserving the distinction between a spatial analysis and an independent measurement network.
- **Sources:** Checksum-cached 32 official MeteoSwiss NetCDF assets from collection `ch.meteoschweiz.ogd-surface-derived-grid`: annual TabsM v1.4 and RhiresM v2.0 grids for 2013–2025 plus monthly grids for January–March 2026. Also cached the collection/archive STAC records, recent-item STAC records and official product documents. Every grid is verified against the publisher's STAC multihash before use.
- **Method:** Validated CF-1.8 metadata, product/version, units, 370×240 regular `ch01r.swiss.lv95` geometry, 1 km spacing, time axes and matched temperature/precipitation coordinates. Assigned each of the 15 reviewed component points to its nearest common-valid grid-cell centre, transformed with EPSG:2056, then constructed cell-specific 2013–2025 calendar-month normals and anomalies. Compared these with the existing station anomalies on common complete months and aggregated the unchanged component mapping to 11 destination units. Reused the predeclared station-sensitivity agreement thresholds.
- **Result:** All 15 components use distinct grid cells, the largest point-to-cell-centre distance is 519.10 m, and all 2,385 component-month rows are complete. Twelve comparisons are high, one moderate and two low. Median component correlations are 0.983 for temperature anomalies and 0.942 for precipitation anomalies; median absolute anomaly differences are 0.239 °C and 14.90 mm/month. At destination level, 1,744 of 1,749 rows have both station and grid proxies complete; eight destinations are high, one moderate and two low.
- **Substantive finding:** Axalp and Meiringen-Hasliberg are the two low-agreement destinations because their valley-station temperature anomalies correlate only about 0.75–0.77 with the resort-grid signal, with still lower winter correlations. Schwanden is moderate. These results expose a representativeness problem that the aggregate medians would otherwise hide; they do not justify silently replacing station observations or treating the grid as piste weather.
- **Limits:** TabsM uses homogenised station monthly means and RhiresM uses rain-gauge monthly totals with spatial interpolation. The 1 km spacing is not effective 1 km precipitation resolution; high-alpine interpolation, winter undercatch and piste microclimate remain unresolved. Because both products are informed by the MeteoSwiss observing network, the comparison is a gridded-product sensitivity, not a genuinely independent measurement-network validation or causal-covariate approval.
- **Problems encountered:** The originally catalogued RhiresM documentation URL returned HTTP 404; the failed attempt remains in the append-only collection log and the configuration now uses the current official document URL. The NetCDF C library could not open the repository's Unicode Windows path directly; the builder therefore passes verified file bytes through the supported in-memory `netCDF4.Dataset` interface, preserving identical source hashes and read-only handling.
- **Decision:** Adopt Path C v0.17 with `swissmetnet_station_vs_meteoswiss_tabsm_rhiresm_1km_gridded_monthly_sensitivity_not_independent_measurement_network_piste_microclimate_or_causal_approval`. Keep all gridded-weather causal approvals false and leave uplift, accessibility and composite scores blank.
- **Validation:** The complete offline pipeline regenerated all dependent outputs. All 44 automated tests pass, including exact 32-asset, 15-cell, 2,385-row and 1,744 paired-destination-month grain; official-hash enforcement; agreement classifications; and protected-output gates. Provenance contains 135 source records and the data dictionary contains 2,090 table-variable rows.
- **Next step:** Prioritise an independent gridded snow sensitivity. Continue the separate destination-catchment, authorised historical-GTFS, historical competing-pass and dated-infrastructure gates. Add a genuinely independent weather measurement or reanalysis source only if regional weather is promoted from descriptive sensitivity to a causal covariate.

## 2026-09-24 03:56 CEST — SPASS 1 km daily gridded-snow sensitivity

- **Task:** Test whether the selected SLF IMIS snow-station proxy is robust to a peer-reviewed 1 km daily snow-depth grid and distinguish model/measurement disagreement from station-to-resort spatial mismatch.
- **Source:** Collected WSL/SLF and MeteoSwiss SPASS v1.0 `HSCLQMD`, EnviDat DOI `10.16904/envidat.580`, with methods published at `10.5194/tc-19-4391-2025`. The archive contains one NetCDF member covering hydrological years 1962–2023; partial annual extraction is therefore impossible.
- **Collection and integrity:** Implemented a resumable 64 MiB HTTP byte-range collector after the initial Python `urllib` request path stalled before usable headers. The complete ZIP is 1,815,467,625 bytes with ETag `"ce7a15403ac83bf1977cec1e2e1b2188-70"` and SHA-256 `eb2ca59883add401883a665337653b103afde02cb57bb12d313b9fb776d4c84d`. Its sole member has CRC32 `fcb899d7`, expands to 8,882,362,459 bytes and has SHA-256 `b8d740993a4bfa2b542aa2c2986564073d275b07f2b2fd9c869dfc5daf597c0d`. Sizes, ETag, member name, CRC32 and both hashes are pinned in `config/spass_snow_grid.json` and revalidated before processing.
- **Method:** Validated CF-1.8, the 370×265 regular 1 km LV95 grid, product/version, hydrological time axis and physical value bounds. For each of 15 reviewed resort components, extracted the nearest cell and daily median of the surrounding 3×3 cells both at the resort point and at the selected SLF station location. Aggregated November–April depth and ≥30 cm shares for ten common winters, 2013/14–2022/23, then aggregated unique component/station signals to the unchanged 11 reviewed destination units. Agreement rules match the predeclared next-nearest-station sensitivity.
- **Result:** All 150 component-winters and all 110 destination-winters are complete. Resort-centred 3×3 agreement is high for one component, moderate for six and low for eight; median winter-depth correlation is 0.887 and median relative mean absolute depth difference is 61.9%. When the same 3×3 grid method is centred at the selected station, agreement is high for nine and moderate for six, with median correlation 0.897 and relative difference 25.9%. Destination agreement is high for one, moderate for four and low for six, with median correlation 0.906 and relative difference 60.8%.
- **Substantive finding:** The strong improvement at the station location shows that the main weakness is station-to-resort placement/elevation representativeness rather than method alone. The comparison must not be read as proving that either source is true piste snow: IMIS stations are local flat sites, while SPASS is an areal model that smooths unresolved terrain and land cover.
- **Limits:** SPASS OSHD-CLQM is forced by gridded MeteoSwiss fields, quantile-mapped to the snow-assimilating OSHD-EKF target and converted from SWE with SWE2HS. It is therefore not an independent measurement network. It does not observe piste snow, snowmaking, grooming, wind redistribution or operating days, and it ends in August 2023. The distributed `HSCLQMD` variable has no `units` attribute; raw values are explicitly interpreted as metres and converted to centimetres based on their physical magnitude and the peer-reviewed definition of HS analyses in centimetres. This upstream metadata omission remains visible in every report.
- **Problems encountered:** The NetCDF C library could not open the repository's Unicode Windows path reliably. The builder creates a verified temporary ASCII-path hard link, reads the immutable file there and removes only the link afterward. The large source is rehashed on every analytical run rather than trusting filenames or prior metadata.
- **Decision:** Adopt Path C v0.18 with `slf_imis_station_vs_spass_oshd_clqm_1km_modelled_snow_sensitivity_shared_slf_assimilation_target_not_independent_measurement_network_piste_observation_or_causal_approval`. Keep every snow causal-approval field false. Seek an independent measurement/reanalysis source only if snow is later proposed as a causal covariate.
- **Validation:** The complete offline pipeline regenerated all dependent outputs and every Path C summary at v0.18. All 45 automated tests pass. Provenance contains 137 source records and the data dictionary contains 2,229 table-variable rows, including 139 rows across the four SPASS-derived tables.
- **Next step:** Continue the higher-value open evidence gates: defensible destination/catchment tourism dependence, authorised historical GTFS comparison, historical competing-pass reconstruction and dated infrastructure. Preserve the present non-causal Path C architecture unless a redesigned donor/exposure strategy passes the failed causal gates.

## 2026-09-24 10:19 CEST — Component-point versus reviewed outcome-scope concordance

- **Validation:** Added a pipeline stage, versioned configuration, a 30-field audit table, summary JSON, report 37 and automated invariants for class separation, exact/link counts, the Meiringen omission and all false approval gates. The complete offline pipeline passes all 46 tests. Provenance contains 138 source records and the data dictionary contains 2,286 table-variable rows.

## 2026-10-02 — Accessibility expansion completed for the initial descriptive test

- **Routing:** Completed 316,944 timetable requests (71 groups × 186 municipal series × 24 departure/return samples) against the checksum-pinned winter-2026 GTFS feed. Eighty-five municipal series and 57 station groups have at least one raw pair within 120 minutes. All 142 group/day caches are resumable.

Four sensitivity thresholds (30/60/90/120 minutes) retain active-group counts, new entries, known structural sums and the nearest active group. The primary entry anchor is November 2017 for founders and May of the later entry season; a separate winter anchor begins in November and applies only from November through April. Crans-Montana's documented exit after April 2020 is applied; continuity for other groups is an explicit assumption.
- **Multiple-entry design:** A municipality may be exposed to several groups simultaneously. The event table contains 28,178 group–municipality–relative-month rows across 386 accessible pair-entry combinations. Fifty-eight municipalities experience multiple active groups within 120 minutes at least once, with a maximum of 20. Sixteen groups have some municipality where their entry is separated by more than 12 months from another entry.
- **Feasibility:** Thirty-four groups have at least one pair with 24 observed months before and after entry and therefore receive an adjacent 24-month descriptive diagnostic. Twenty-eight have a positive median raw log change, but this is not interpreted as an effect: trends, COVID, contemporaneous entries, seasonality and selection remain unresolved. Zero causal treatments, controls or effects are approved.

## 2026-10-02 — Expanded exposure identification and pretrend screening

- **Task:** Continue beyond descriptive feasibility by checking whether the 34 station groups with at least one complete 24/24 pair can support separable effects, and whether a municipality-level alternative has a plausible comparison pool.
- **Station-effect audit:** Among 277 complete group–municipality windows, 185 overlap March 2020–April 2022. Only 15 pairs across Schwarzsee, Tramelan and Villars-Gryon-Les Diablerets combine complete 24/24 outcomes, no COVID overlap and no other accessible entry within twelve months. Even these remain unapproved because current 2026 access is projected historically and no comparison is validated.
- **Collinearity result:** At 120 minutes, 34 nonzero station-effect columns have rank 31 after municipality and month fixed effects. Anzère/Mayens de Conthey, Grimentz-Zinal/St-Luc–Chandolin and Aeschiallmend/Faltschen each share the same anchor and exact current catchment. Individual effects for each pair are algebraically inseparable under this exposure definition.
- **Sequential-entry result:** Eighty municipalities have at least one usable 120-minute exposure, 46 have several distinct entry months and 27 have two complete event windows. Zero municipalities have two entry windows that are both isolated and outside COVID. Sequential entries remain useful descriptively but do not identify station-specific causal effects.
- **Alternative design:** Defined first accessible exposure to the Magic Pass network as the treatment event. Nineteen founding-cohort municipalities have complete 24/24 outcomes, no COVID and no later accessible entry during the post 24 months. The same window provides 132 mechanically unexposed comparisons, including 99 never exposed in the current matrix.
- **Pre-period screen:** Compared the 19 treated municipalities with all 132 candidates over 36 pre-anchor months, creating 2,508 profiles. Five relative matches per treatment yield 95 pairs and 50 distinct comparison municipalities. Median shortlisted pre-period log-outcome correlation is 0.803 and the median absolute deseasonalised monthly trend gap is 0.0029. These metrics prioritise manual review and do not establish parallel trends.
- **Outputs:** Added reports 58–59, pair/group/municipality/cohort/overlap identification audits, the first-exposure registry, complete pretrend pair table, top-five shortlist and machine-readable summaries. The expansion runner now rebuilds both steps.
- **Provenance and guardrail:** Registered one additional derived source and 173 net new table-variable definitions; the registers now contain 170 sources and 3,197 dictionary rows. Historical access, local shocks, competing passes and spillovers remain pending. Zero causal treatments, controls, parallel-trend approvals or effects are produced.

National day networks and group/day route outputs are checkpointed separately. The older GTFS contains one temporally invalid trip on each sampled day; both are excluded without correction or imputation. Its transfer table lacks the optional route/trip scope columns, which are treated as absent rather than fabricated.
- **Strict negative result:** The published 2018 transfer table reduces to only 184 normalized cross-stop links, versus 7,369 in the official 2026 feed. Consequently only 9/39 pairs have all 24 strict itineraries and only 3/39 remain within 120 minutes. This result is retained as a documented under-connected lower bound, not interpreted as a collapse in real accessibility.
- **250-metre sensitivity:** Added bidirectional modelled walking links only between active 2018 GTFS stops no more than 250 m apart, with 120 seconds interchange plus walking at 1.2 m/s rounded upward by 30 seconds. This adds 17,688 directed links on Wednesday and 16,926 on Saturday, completes 39/39 routes, keeps 22/39 within 120 minutes and retains at least one founding group for 13/19 municipalities. Because it creates more links than the 2026 official graph, it is retained as a permissive sensitivity rather than the preferred historical reconstruction.
- **Current-topology sensitivity:** Projected only official 2026 cross-stop transfer links whose two normalized IDs are already active in the 2018 day network, preserving all 2018 links. This adds 4,714 links on Wednesday and 4,658 on Saturday, completes 39/39 routes and keeps 24/39 within 120 minutes. Fourteen of 19 municipalities retain at least one founding group; six retain every group assigned by the current 120-minute matrix. Burgdorf, Thun, Murten, Orsières and Champéry do not retain a founding exposure under this sensitivity.
- **Decision:** Keep all three scenarios side by side. Use the current-topology projection only as the most directly documented structural sensitivity, never as proof that the same transfer link was formally published in 2018. Do not approve a causal treatment, control, parallel-trend assumption or effect. Historical timetable plausibility is improved for 14 municipalities, but local shocks, spillovers, comparison review and the sensitivity choice remain unresolved.
- **Outputs:** Reports 61–63, three 936-row request tables, three 39-row context tables, two sensitivity municipality validations, the 31-row stop audit, updated worklist, configuration, caches, tests and provenance registration.
- **Next step:** Manually review the 14 historically retained treated municipalities and their 95 pre-period comparison pairs for municipality-specific shocks, spillovers and outcome-scope validity. Treat the five non-retained municipalities as failed 120-minute sensitivity cases unless a separately justified access rule is adopted.

## 2026-10-03 — Operational tourist-basin and direct-local-spillover screen

- **Task:** Translate the researcher's proposed municipality-centre-to-station travel-time concept into a reproducible basin definition for the 21 stage-21 pairs.
- **Rule:** A direct local basin is the set of municipality reference origins within 120 mean scheduled minutes of an entering station group. Pair-centre distance above 50 km, control distance above 50 km from every window-active Magic access point, no shared current territory and non-exposure during November 2015–October 2019 are supporting conditions. Official destination boundaries are not hard gates because they do not establish actual visitor catchments.
- **Result:** All 21 pairs exceed 50 km between centres; the minimum is 78.1 km. Twenty controls have a complete current route to at least one entering group and the minimum is 138.2 minutes. Vitznau–Saignelégier has no complete specific route to Tramelan but Vitznau remains outside 120 minutes in the full window matrix. All 21 advance to the pretrend diagnostic as low direct-local-risk cases.
- **Guardrail:** Broad national or interregional visitor substitution remains unresolved for every pair. Current scheduled times are a sensitivity, not exact 2017 routes. No spillover absence, parallel trend, causal control or causal pair is approved.

## 2026-10-03 — Predeclared pair-level pretrend diagnostics

- **Task:** Apply magnitude-based pre-treatment compatibility rules before any post-treatment estimation.
- **Rule:** The checksum-recorded primary rule requires 36 common months, log-outcome correlation at least 0.70, standardized RMSE at most 0.80, seasonal-profile RMSE at most 0.25 and absolute seasonally adjusted differential trend at most 0.005 log points per month. The relaxed sensitivity uses 0.60, 0.90, 0.30 and 0.0075. A Newey-West covariance with 12 lags reports trend uncertainty, but non-rejection alone never passes a pair.
- **Result:** Eleven pairs pass the primary rule and cover six treated municipalities. Four additional La Chaux-de-Fonds pairs pass only the relaxed sensitivity. Six pairs fail even the relaxed rule: Bulle–Vitznau, Gruyères–Rorschacherberg and all four Haut-Intyamon pairs. Haut-Intyamon therefore has no current estimation candidate.
- **Guardrail:** Passing means pre-period compatibility under disclosed rules, not proof of parallel trends. Historical access still depends on projected 2026 transfer topology over active 2018 stops, and broad tourism-market substitution remains unresolved. All parallel-trend and causal approvals remain false.
- **Outputs:** Report 70, 21-row diagnostic table, 70-row stage-23 screen, 15-row primary-plus-relaxed estimation candidate file, rule configuration and JSON summary. Provenance contains 216 sources and 5,080 dictionary rows.
- **Validation:** Six targeted stage-21-to-stage-23 tests pass, followed by the complete project suite (`80 passed`). Milestone commit: `bc0a90d`.
- **Resume point:** Build a clearly labelled non-causal pilot from the 11 primary pairs, keeping the four relaxed pairs separate and applying inherited leave-one-control/post-window sensitivities. Account for repeated municipalities and do not claim a causal Magic Pass effect.

## 2026-10-03 — Weighted matched-stack non-causal pilot

- **Task:** Run the first quantitative post-screen test without converting pretrend compatibility into a causal claim.
- **Predeclared estimator:** Eleven primary pairs form six matched stacks. Within every stack-month, the treated municipality has weight one and all controls together have weight one. A two-way within transformation removes stack-by-municipality and stack-by-calendar-month effects. Sandwich scores are clustered by stable BFS municipality across repeated control appearances, with a finite-cluster G/(G-1) correction and normal 95% intervals. The outcome is log hotel overnights over event months -24 to +23.
- **Primary result:** The weighted pilot coefficient is -0.0706 log points, or -6.8%, with a normal municipality-clustered interval from -11.7% to -1.6%. Independent arithmetic reproduces it exactly as the equal mean of six treated-stack post-minus-pre gaps.
- **Sensitivity:** Uniformly ending the post window at event month 13 gives -7.2%. Adding the four relaxed-only pairs gives -8.7%. Removing all controls with targeted timing or leave-out notes leaves five pairs/four treatments and gives -2.0%, with an interval from -7.8% to +4.3%. Leave-one-pair point estimates range from -8.8% to -5.7%; leave-one-treated estimates range from -8.8% to -4.8%.
- **Heterogeneity:** Treated-stack changes range from -16.4% for Saignelégier to +3.5% for La Chaux-de-Fonds. Bulle is -12.7%, Köniz -11.2%, Fribourg -2.0% and Gruyères -0.4%. The direction is therefore not uniform.
- **Historical-access cross-check:** All six primary treated municipalities are retained under both the projected-current-topology and separate 250 m proximity-transfer sensitivities. None is retained with the strict published 2018 transfer table alone because that feed exposes only 184 normalized cross-stop links. Agreement across augmented scenarios improves plausibility but does not verify the historical transfer graph.
- **Guardrail:** The pilot has only 16 unique municipality clusters, broad visitor substitution remains unresolved, historical routing is sensitivity-based and the screened sample is selected. The -6.8% result is not an identified Magic Pass effect. Every causal approval and causal-claim flag remains false.
- **Outputs:** Report 71, the 1,824-row primary-plus-relaxed stacked panel, four specification estimates, sixteen binned event coefficients, seventeen influence runs, six treated-stack decompositions, predeclared configuration and JSON summary. Provenance contains 217 sources and 5,175 dictionary rows.
- **Validation:** Four pilot-specific tests and the complete project suite (`84 passed`) verify balanced windows, within-stack weights, exact arithmetic replication, sensitivity counts and protected false causal flags. Milestone commit: `1cf4cb9`.
- **Resume point:** Present report 71 as exploratory evidence only. If the thesis requires stronger inference, prioritise historical interchange validation for the six primary treatments and a small-cluster inference sensitivity; otherwise integrate the conservative result and limitations into the thesis narrative.

## 2026-10-04 — Small-cluster inference and thesis integration

- **Task:** Test whether the stage-24 pilot's apparent precision survives an inference reference adapted to the small number of effectively treated municipalities, then integrate the defensible conclusion into the thesis-facing methodological synthesis.
- **Predeclared checks:** The versioned stage-25 config fixes two diagnostics before execution. First, the six treated-stack post-minus-pre contrasts are treated as the effective observations for a Student-t reference with five degrees of freedom. Second, stacks sharing any municipality are joined by transitive closure and every sign pattern is enumerated over the resulting disconnected components. The sign diagnostic assumes component-level symmetry under the null and is explicitly not random-assignment inference.
- **Dependence structure:** The stage-24 coefficient is exactly the mean of six treated-stack contrasts. Emmen (BFS 1024) is reused by Bulle and Gruyères, so the six stacks reduce to five disconnected stack-municipality components. All 32 joint sign configurations are enumerated.
- **Result:** The original normal municipality-clustered reference gives p=0.010. The treated-stack t reference gives p=0.099 and a 95% interval from -14.8% to +1.9%. The five-component exact sign sensitivity gives p=0.188; with 32 patterns its minimum attainable two-sided p-value is 0.0625. The negative -6.8% mean therefore is not robustly distinguishable from zero under the small-effective-sample checks.
- **Interpretation:** This strengthens the existing caution rather than the treatment claim. Together with the -2.0% conservative control-exclusion sensitivity and heterogeneous treated-stack estimates, the result supports only an exploratory matched-comparison statement. Zero causal approvals or causal-effect claims are introduced.
- **Methodological sources:** Registered Canay, Romano and Shaikh (2017) for approximate sign-change tests under symmetry and MacKinnon and Webb (2018) for the limits of inference with few treated clusters. Report 72 records the procedure, assumptions and files.
- **Thesis integration:** Updated the mission-required methodological report, executive summary, thesis reading draft and detailed review draft so questions on parallel trends, causal credibility and heterogeneity reflect stages 23–25. The final classification remains exploratory decision support, not causal or predictive uplift.
- **Outputs:** `config/accessibility_small_cluster_inference.json`, the stage-25 builder, report 72, the inference and component CSVs, summary JSON, runner/provenance integration and four focused tests. Provenance contains 220 sources and 5,212 dictionary rows.
- **Validation:** All 88 project tests pass after regenerating the methodological report, executive summary and thesis reading drafts.
- **Milestone commit:** `de49e32` (`research: audit small-cluster pilot inference`). Mixed pre-existing editorial files remain outside this commit.
- **Resume point:** Keep the pilot frozen as exploratory evidence unless new historical interchange data or a broader independent donor design becomes available. The next work should be thesis wording/typesetting rather than additional modelling that does not change the claim.

## 2026-10-05 — Critical read-only diagnosis after 120-minute catchment challenge

- **Researcher concern:** The 120-minute rule is too lax and does not identify communes whose hotel nights plausibly depend on Magic Pass stations. Diagnose without silently changing a methodological threshold or filtering communes.
- **Scope evidence:** Existing all-cohort coverage and route-context files show first-exposure links Lancy–Saint-Cergue 77.1 minutes, Meyrin–Saint-Cergue 93.9, Kriens–Marbachegg 117.5, Bourg-en-Lavaux–Villars/Gryon/Diablerets 81.9. These are current sampled scheduled generalized transport times including waiting, not demonstrated lodging catchments. Tighter existing thresholds yield 5/30/55/79 distinct exposed territories at 30/60/90/120 minutes after the exact Moutier identity bridge; eight commune first-exposure dates change between 120 and 60. These inventories are not valid replacements until routing-definition issues are resolved.
- **Concrete coding issue:** `DestinationProfile.query_number` correctly returns a zero-leg, zero-duration journey for identical origin/destination stops. `sample_row` in `src/data_processing/build_public_transport_journey_context.py` requires `0 < journey.legs`, rejecting it as `no_vehicle_leg_same_stop_or_walk_only`. Joining current station access nodes to municipality origins finds three group/commune same-node cases: Leukerbad (Busterminal, official-map point 175 m away), Hasliberg (Wasserwendi Twing, point 43 m away), and one Beatenberg/Niederhorn access node (Waldegg, point 655 m away). Group aggregate routes are missing for Leukerbad and Hasliberg; Beatenberg retains a route via another access point, but its same-node option is still rejected. These point distances are evidence of the node relationship, not fabricated walking travel times.
- **Consequences:** Leukerbad's own group enters 2019-05, but the commune first exposure is coded 2023-05 through Eischoll at 114 minutes. Hasliberg is coded donor-never-exposed across all seven approved cohort universes despite the inaccessible own local group route. Catchment tightening alone cannot recover these omissions and may turn omitted local exposure into additional erroneous control classifications. Tests established consistency with existing coding rules, not scientific validity of those rules.
- **Status and provenance:** No external source or analytical variable created; all findings come from registered local route/node/group/cohort files and source code. One read-only point lookup initially failed because the access-point table has group IDs but no group-name column; repaired the diagnostic lookup by joining the existing stable group IDs. No source data, config, cached routing, exposure, estimate or figure was altered. Only state/journal record the issue for continuation. Preserve reports 82/83 but flag their exposure timing and interpretation as pending reassessment.
- **Next decision:** Explain options and obtain approval before treating same-node or walk-only paths as local accessibility, defining walking/last-mile assumptions, choosing transport modes, and changing the main threshold.
